- Every folded item is named inside its grouped row's Statement, so none is skipped; every grouped row of kind "claim" and destination DROP names the earlier VL ids it duplicates.

### What the ledger says, in short

1. Collaboration value is unproven everywhere. No package (R1 to r5) has Spike 4 values or a non-smoke arm row; the measured rows are single smoke runs on the 2-item control package or one package (VL-22-065, VL-22-276, VL-22-902). The only strong measured effect is a cost: r5's static catalogue kit alone raised sonnet elapsed time by 59% and output tokens by 55% (VL-22-864).
2. CORE content that two or more modules would need, and that all five designs rebuilt separately: the finding contract and its normaliser (VL-22-045, VL-22-047, VL-22-325, VL-22-554, VL-22-598, VL-22-914), merge and its non-idempotence (VL-22-243, VL-22-316, VL-22-630), reviewer identity and resolution (VL-22-040, VL-22-046, VL-22-899, VL-22-908), dispatch (read-only launch, model allowlist and no fallback, isolated retry, one deadline rule with a hard hang guard because the finish signal is advisory, resume and session-id capture: VL-22-025, VL-22-027, VL-22-141, VL-22-326, VL-22-327, VL-22-372, VL-22-448, VL-22-474, VL-22-475, VL-22-613, VL-22-703, VL-22-782), the seal and telemetry contracts (VL-22-017, VL-22-018, VL-22-328, VL-22-472, VL-22-699, VL-22-825), run-record and return-contract fields (VL-22-028, VL-22-102, VL-22-103, VL-22-268, VL-22-318, VL-22-319, VL-22-369, VL-22-595, VL-22-631, VL-22-746, VL-22-795, VL-22-879), the model capability table (VL-22-324, VL-22-532) and the transport abstraction (VL-22-910, VL-22-911).
3. MODULE-tasklist holds all five collaboration families (move radar, sweeper, axis-flip challenge, flag-and-adjudicate, coverage deflection), their message kinds, validators, catalogues and role prompts. They are tasklist-specific (`## Task <n>:` headings, plan criteria) and need new catalogues and coverage units for MODULE-pr-review (VL-22-346, VL-22-882, VL-22-918, VL-22-333) and MODULE-rfqa (VL-22-345); MODULE-reflect gets the hook-reach limit and the restricted-tool reviewer fact (VL-22-909, VL-22-921, VL-22-922, VL-22-941). MODULE-spec has no row of its own: neither slice contains a spec-review-specific item (the M1-M10 catalogue could be reused, noted in VL-22-044 and VL-22-198).
4. EVAL (288 rows) is the largest group: ground truth, judge pins, anonymiser, audits, alarms, planning numbers, gate results, smoke rows and acceptance evidence. HARNESS (173) is experiment scripts and tests. DROP (237) is mostly duplicates inside the two slices (each names the retained row) plus size inventories and slice reading gaps.
5. Defects that matter for reuse: `.gitignore:13` (`lib/`) hides the engine of R2, r3, r4 and r5 (VL-22-167, VL-22-530, VL-22-697, VL-22-840, VL-22-904); the turn-measurement defect that R1 fixed is still in R2 and, newly found by me, in r3's `turn_durations` (VL-22-287, VL-22-288, VL-22-955); r5's sandbox builder lacks `-f` (VL-22-884); acceptance by `git status`/`find -newer` is unsound (VL-22-338, VL-22-513).

## Gaps and Questions

### Gaps (every item below is [UNVERIFIED] or limits a row)

1. [UNVERIFIED] Claimed passing tests: R1 138, R2 358, r3 89, r4 106, r5 108 (799 claimed in total across the five packages); I ran no test and no model. Static counts (above) differ from the claims for R1, R2, r4 and r5, so the claims cannot be confirmed from the tip.
2. [UNVERIFIED] Every planning, timing and measured number taken from a slice (planning B, k, finish offsets, act rates, smoke rows, hook latency, token counts, spike counts, file counts) is slice-sourced; only the items in the verification table were re-derived or re-read. Rows carry the tag where it matters.
3. [UNVERIFIED] The requirement source `merged-requirements.md` (R-01..R-47, failure modes F1 to F8), the run-1 harness files cited by the designs (`run-reviewer.sh:48`, `judge-one.sh`, `merge-b11.sh`, `final-table.py`, `SPEC.md`) and the E2 worktree on disk (where the missing `lib/` may still exist) were not read by me or by the slices; every R-number, REQ line citation and "F1..F7" definition in the ledger is second-hand.
4. [UNVERIFIED] Destination assignments are provisional by assignment ("use these destination rules now; the synthesis re-validates them"). Judgement calls that could move: collaboration mechanisms (MODULE-tasklist versus CORE if a second module adopts them), hook/seal/telemetry rows (CORE), the capability and act-rate facts (EVAL versus CORE), sandbox rows (HARNESS versus MODULE-pr-review).
5. Granularity limit: the grouped rows listed in the Summary fold several slice items into one row (named inside the Statement); a downstream reader who needs per-scenario rows for the 25 + 19 + 17 fault scenarios, the S9.1 matrix or the V-01..V-74 inventory must expand them from the named VL ids.
6. MODULE-spec has 0 rows because neither slice contains a spec-review-specific item; this is a property of the sources, not a decision to drop spec.
7. R2's `bin/lib/` (validator, renderer, common.sh) and r3/r4/r5 engines cannot be inspected; the validator rules and the renderer template are retained only as design rows (VL-22-169 to VL-22-179, VL-22-467, VL-22-623, VL-22-770).
8. The reviewer id of the haiku reviewer in r5 hl2 (`t4-1`) differs from the other designs (`t2-1`); I kept the slice's text (VL-22-785, VL-22-899) without checking r5's `panels.json`.
9. Independence: the run-1 folder and the queued-run note named in shared rule R2 were not read, listed, grepped or cited; the only task-folder files read are the two assigned slices and line 215 of the task file; `.dev/tasks/` was excluded from repo-wide searches (all searches were pinned to `src/superclaude/cli/review`, `src/superclaude/cli/reflect`, `src/superclaude/agents/reflect-reviewer.md` or `git show`/`git ls-tree` on the E2 tip).

### Questions (for the synthesis)

1. Should the unified core adopt `review-panel-resolution/1` from `panel.py` for reviewer identity instead of the per-design jq rewrites (VL-22-908), and should reviewer ids be treated as labels with an explicit id-to-role table (VL-22-899)?
2. Which deadline rule does the core use when there is no per-input baseline (VL-22-344): static per-model budgets, a calibration table, or a hard hang guard only (the finish signal is advisory: VL-22-703)?
3. Does the unified design keep any collaboration module at all, given zero quality evidence and a measured prompt-side cost (VL-22-864, VL-22-902), or park the five families as MODULE-tasklist backlog behind the CORE seal, telemetry and message-envelope contracts?
4. Where does the `origin` own/peer tag live (in-line key tolerated by v1 and apparently by v2 at HEAD, versus sidecar: VL-22-366, VL-22-607)?
5. Which turn-measurement reducer is canonical (R1 `turns_of`, VL-22-120) and who replaces the gap-method copies in R2 and r3 (VL-22-287, VL-22-955)?
6. Can E1's shadow-run data substitute part of the missing Spike 4 calibration (VL-22-947)?
7. Is ground-truth scoring a study-only layer, with production modules (PR review, spec review, rf-qa) using a different run record (VL-22-952, VL-22-919)?
8. Can reflect Tier 2 reviewers be hooked at all, or is the resume-based transport (VL-22-910) the only collaboration path that reaches them (VL-22-941)?
9. Who recovers or re-derives the missing engines (R2 `bin/lib/`, r3/r4/r5 `lib/`) and who changes the `lib/` ignore rule (VL-22-904, VL-22-943)? Out of scope for this report-only task.
